## Consequential reading corrections

- Scan 003, Principal line 10, both limits around dérigés: `\varprojlim^{(i)} ... \varprojlim`. Each lim has a separately drawn arrow underneath with a shaft extending rightward and an unambiguous left arrowhead. These are not mere underlines.

- Scan 004, Main lines 13–14, T(f) formula across the line break: `T(f):T(\longrightarrow T(Y)`. The first line ends after T followed by an opening parenthesis; neither X nor a closing parenthesis is visible. The next line begins directly with a handwritten right arrow and T(Y). The complete surviving formula was inspected in both adjacent fragments.

The source has T prime, colon, (Ssimpl) prime, a short hand-drawn right arrow, opening parenthesis and C. No a, t, closing parenthesis or following prime survives. The separate S-prime formula earlier in this line is complete.

At the end of the ring subscript X is a tiny filled circular return, compatible with a lower 0 but also with the returning pen stroke of X. Prefer X0 with X retained. The map from f has a left bar and full right head; the later horizontal stroke between the ring and A has no head, so it remains a minus. The raised star remains locally doubtful.
- Scan 022, Ring subscript of the underlined Hom: `\underline O_{\unsure{X_0&#124;X}}`. Both ring subscripts have crossed X strokes followed by a tiny upturned closed or filled return. The return may be a separate 0 but is not cleanly separable from X, so retain X0/X at both places. Hom and D each have their underline. The two-loop D form follows the established script-D alphabet; alpha and upper category C are distinct.
- Scan 022, Lower index of the first script D after Alors: `\mathscr D_{\unsure{0&#124;\mathcal C&#124;\mathrm g}}^{\mathcal C}`. The first D has a lower closed loop with a short pointed return. It favours 0, but a curled C or g can fit; add g to the retained alternatives. The line under lim is plain, without an arrowhead, and alpha is beneath it. Keep underlined lim, not a directed-limit arrow.
- Scan 022, Decoration under lim: `\underline{\lim}_\alpha`. A separate lower loop sits under the first D and is not absent; it favors 0 but could be a tightly curled category C. The bar below lim has no secure two-sided arrowhead; alpha lies beneath it. The upper C and lower alpha of the last D are distinct.
- Scan 023, L3, initial symbol of full display: `\mathfrak g^{\mathcal C}`. Upper hook and long returning lower descender identify g; no T top bar. This is a letter change, not merely a font choice.
- Scan 023, L3, lower index of second Omega: `G_\alpha`. The main loop has an internal horizontal bar absent from the open C forms; small alpha follows below/right. C first reading is retained with certainty increased.
- Scan 023, L3, live upper index of second Omega: `\Omega_{G_\alpha}^{\mathcal C}`. An open curved C stands above/right of the second Omega without a crossing cancellation.
- Scan 023, L5, noun following sa: `déduction`. After the accented first d/e sequence there is a second tall d and a low u-c-t group; these do not match r-i-v-a. The ending i/on is compressed.
- Scan 023, L9, small raised insertion after Module: `\inserted{\unsure{qu.&#124;q.} coh}`. The first abbreviation has a clear q descender and a long flattened right return that can contain u or be q’s exit stroke; keep qu./q. Both the stop after it and coh without a final stop are visible. The insertion leader rises from after Module, crosses the space before Omega and ends in a small upper return; refine that final hook while leaving all letters outside the drawing.
- Scan 023, L17, target glyph of the second map: `\overset{2}{A}\Omega`. A distinct horizontal crossbar projects through the left leg and stops within the triangular target glyph. This is a written A, unlike the unbarred wedge in B020. Resolve the earlier A/wedge doubt to the literal A and center the raised 2 above its apex. The exterior-square interpretation belongs in the notes, not a substituted glyph.
- Scan 024, Cancelled prefix beneath anti: `\deleted{h\unsure{omo&#124;om}}`. The initial cancelled h has an exposed ascender and shoulder. The following round bodies are obscured, with homo preferred but the final o not separable from the strike; retain omo/om. Above it, anti is clear. Its reinforced underline turns downward at the right into a distinct hook. Draw that connected underline/attachment, with anti as ordinary text.
- Scan 024, Top insertion, lower cancelled reference: `\deleted{\unsure{\beta)}}`. Long stem, crowded double-loop bodies and curved right stroke lie under upper references. Overlapping cancellation/replacement prevents secure separation.
- Scan 024, Top insertion, raised references: `\unsure{\alpha)&#124;\alpha/}\,\beta)`. The first upper sign has an alpha-like loop and crossing, followed by an oblique terminal that can be a compressed closing parenthesis or slash. The following beta and right parenthesis are distinct. Retain alpha)/alpha/ as whole local alternatives; this shared insertion was applied with B004.
- Scan 024, Second body line after et cette: `dernière`. d/ern run, two small upper marks, compact i/è/re ending; no independent t stem/crossbar or -ion tail. Compare much longer first-line dérivation.
- Scan 024, Module letter after Soit: `\mathfrak g`. Left U-shaped bowl and long right stem returning into lower loop, unlike a T cap; repeats in three later occurrences.
- Scan 024, Module letter after sections de: `\mathfrak g`. Open upper bowl and curled right descender repeat defining g; no T cap.
- Scan 024, Displayed section, cancelled argument: `\deleted{(\omega)}`. Two adjacent rounded lower lobes survive between curved parentheses beneath diagonal masks; U would have one bowl.
- Scan 024, Module letter in Gamma: `\mathfrak g`. Curled right descender and open upper-left bowl match defining g.
- Scan 024, Defining pairing, cancelled argument: `\deleted{\unsure{(\omega)}}`. Curved boundary traces and dense double-bodied middle survive beside reinforced closing angle; intersections prevent secure separation.
- Scan 024, First corrected sign: `\unsure{\deleted{-}+&#124;+&#124;\deleted{+}-}`. The same two dark points precede delta-omega. At the following sign, a tall plus stem crosses a dense horizontal patch; minus changed to plus is preferred, but plain plus and reversed cancellation order cannot be excluded. State whole alternatives rather than asserting an impression chronology. The separate later minus is above the cancelled plus, as already transcribed.
- Scan 024, Second corrected sign: `\overset{\inserted{-}}{\deleted{+}}`. Lower masked body retains vertical/transverse plus limbs. Separate short minus stands above with clear gap.
- Scan 024, Equation tags and final reference: `(x); (xx); (xx)`. Crossing/looped x strokes have no extra radial arms. Double label and final reference have two separate crosses. Labels physically lie at left.
- Scan 024, Action sentence, module letter: `\mathfrak g`. Upper-left bowl and long curled right descender repeat defining g.
- Scan 024, Action sentence, base index: `\underline O_{X_0}`. Separate small closed round 0 below-right of X, distinct from underline beneath O.
- Scan 024, Leibniz annotation reference and closes: `(resulte de \unsure{\alpha)})`. Crowded curved/crossing sign after de meets frame-left. One separate close clear; intersecting stroke could be another close. Alpha and additional close remain joint doubt. No colon at this point or prose close after box.
- Scan 024, Member phrase in On constate paragraph: `mb de (x)`. Low m followed by small b upright/curved body, separate de and parenthesized x. No written -embre tail.
- Scan 024, Cancelled expression before anchor box: `\deleted{\unsure{[X,Y]}}`. Crossing X-like body, separate lower comma and forked Y survive; boundary brackets obscured by horizontal/oblique masks.
- Scan 024, Abbreviation above equality: `\mathrm{df}\unsure{\mathrm{n}}`. d lower oval/upper loop and f tall stem/right exit clear. Final low shoulder could be n or f exit. No separate e between d and f.
- Scan 024, Two retraced product indices: `\unsure{Y&#124;\deleted{X}Y&#124;\deleted{Y}X}; \unsure{Y&#124;\deleted{X}Y&#124;\deleted{Y}X}`. Both lower indices retain upper Y forks but have heavily crossed lower diagonal bands and extra returns. Y remains first; a concealed X and either overwrite direction are additional whole alternatives, with no chronology asserted. The cancelled following f has its tall hooked stem and foot, so the existing f identification is retained. The df/n doubt is shared with B050.
- Scan 024, Cancelled character after first product: `\deleted{f}`. Tall looped stem descends to hooked foot. Several zigzag crossing strokes cancel it; vertical body and upper hook identify f.
- Scan 024, Lower line after f: `est`. Low e curve and joined s/t form a word between f and the separate minus. No mapsto vertical bar, horizontal shaft and head are present.
- Scan 024, Square-zero condition indices: `\delta_1\delta_0=0`. First delta has separate short lowered upright 1; second has separate lowered round 0. Final equation zero is a distinct larger round body.
- Scan 024, Two dark points before delta-omega in the first right-hand pairing: `\langle\unsure{:}\delta\omega,X\wedge Y\rangle`. Two distinct dark points stand between the opening pairing angle and delta-omega; they are also visible in B029 and are substantially darker than the paper texture. Retain a local doubtful colon without assigning it a mathematical purpose. The reinforced closing angle on the left overlaps the old cancelled argument; its boundary is not secure enough to move the cancellation inside the pairing. The tag has crossing x limbs, without extra star arms.

- line3 semi-simplicial, c-like cell: `\unsure{\deleted{v}c&#124;\deleted{c}v}`; alternatives `\unsure{\deleted{v}c&#124;\deleted{c}v}`. One cell contains the round open bowl of c and an internal descending v-form. Record both layers; their chronology remains uncertain.
- line7 second cell after aux: `f\unsure{\deleted{a}o&#124;a}ncteurs`; alternatives `o over a; plain a, hence fancteurs`. Two bowl-like regions and a right baseline foot do not alone determine whether this is a correction or the plain double-storey a.
- line8 qui, underlying glyph: `qu\unsure{\deleted{b}i&#124;\deleted{t}i&#124;\deleted{i}b}`; alternatives `qu\unsure{\deleted{b}i&#124;\deleted{t}i&#124;\deleted{i}b}`. The dotted i and tall hooked upright are separate traces in one cell. A closed middle bowl favours underlying b over t, but the overlap does not prove chronology; retain whole-cell alternatives.
- line11 topos category: `\unsure{C^{\wedge}&#124;C}`; alternatives `\unsure{C^{\wedge}&#124;C}`. Above-right of C are two faint sloping remnants meeting like a caret, not the wave seen on the clear tildes. The mark is weak enough that plain C remains an alternative. No closing parenthesis is visible.
- line15 final cancelled word: `\unsure{Premiers&#124;Exemples}`; alternatives `another P-initial word containing a dotted letter; Exemples not confirmed`. Typed x masks cover the inner letters. The P-like initial and middle dot are the independent positive features; full-word identification is unresolved.
- line16 associer: `asso\unsure{\deleted{v}c&#124;\deleted{c}v}\unsure{\deleted{e}i&#124;\deleted{i}e}r`; alternatives `asso\unsure{\deleted{v}c&#124;\deleted{c}v}\unsure{\deleted{e}i&#124;\deleted{i}e}r`. Preserve both visible layers without certifying their order.
- line18 isomor at right clipping edge: `isomo\unsure{r}`; alternatives `another final upright letter fragment`. Only left serif/upright of last letter is retained; the shoulder is cut. No following hyphen survives.
- line20 ter at right clipping edge: `te\unsure{r}`; alternatives `another final upright letter fragment`. Partial last glyph at the same clipping edge; mes is absent and not treated as uncertain visible ink.
- line21 cancelled equivalence word: `\deleted{e \unsure{équivalence}}`; alternatives `obscured short letters within the same apparent word`. Acute, q descender, i dot and l ascender survive, but x masks hide several remaining letters and edge clips the end.
- line24 first short expression: `\unsure{c.&#124;e.}`; alternatives `e.`. One open rounded letter with a faint possible inner bar; no preceding i or stop is present.
- line24 applications, vowel after c: `applic\unsure{a}tions`; alternatives `unidentified damaged letter`. The a-shaped slot is reduced to disconnected pieces; its full bowl and right stem are not visible.
- line25 cancelled two cells: `\deleted{e\unsure{n&#124;m}}`; alternatives `\deleted{e\unsure{n&#124;m}}`. The first cancelled cell has an e bowl/bar. In the next cell the remnants are upright/arched, favouring n (or m), rather than the two curves of s. Keep that second letter uncertain.
- line4, two overtyped cells in ainsi: `\unsure{\deleted{n}a&#124;\deleted{a}n}\unsure{\deleted{n}i&#124;\deleted{i}n}nsi`; alternatives `\unsure{\deleted{n}a&#124;\deleted{a}n}\unsure{\deleted{n}i&#124;\deleted{i}n}nsi`. - line10, superposed g/v cell: `déri\unsure{\deleted{g}v&#124;\deleted{v}g}és`; alternatives `déri\unsure{\deleted{g}v&#124;\deleted{v}g}és`. ### Unresolved places

- L1/L2 right edge: `faisceau\illegible{1}; dans \illegible{1}`; alternatives `partial t or hyphen at L1; partial u at L2`. The surviving fragments are too cropped to identify complete letters; missing tails are not supplied.
- L3 right edge: `cosimplici\unsure{a}`; alternatives `partial following glyph without secure a`. The left parts of the final cell survive, but its right contour is clipped.
- L15 first cancelled phrase: `\unsure{Il s'en&#124;Il en}`; alternatives `C Il existe`. Only initial I/l and terminal e/n-like remnants are clear. My and root's independent short readings differ.
- L22 insertion: `\unsure{X} et X'`; alternatives `initial X entangled with another crossing stroke`. The first symbol is crossed over; et and the second X′ are clearer.
- L23 right-edge remnant: `U \illegible{1}`; alternatives `de; cancelled unknown continuation`. Only a clipped triangular remnant remains beyond U; word and strike state are not securely recoverable.
- Bottom note, lower line: `\unsure{topologie} \unsure{étale}`; alternatives `no competing secure reading`. Photocopy fading breaks many letters despite C/H agreement.
- Bottom note, after schémas in upper line: `no separate comma`; alternatives `comma joined to terminal s`. The terminal s curl cannot securely be divided into s plus punctuation.
- Bottom note, after par in lower line: `\unsure{schémas&#124;variétés}`; alternatives `deleted unidentified word; H variétés`. Upper-line underline and descenders overlap the lower word; no separate cancellation is secure.
- Bottom note, abbreviated prefix before noeth.: `\unsure{l.}`; alternatives `opening parenthesis; H loc.`. One long stroke and dot are visible, with no clear o/c pair to justify loc.
- L3 cancelled e after inserted F: `\begin{tabular}[b]{@{}l@{}}\inserted{$F$}\\[-.65ex]\begin{tikzpicture}[x=1em,y=1em,baseline=0pt,line width=.45pt]\draw (-.1,-.4)--(.8,.7);\end{tikzpicture}\end{tabular} d\deleted{\unsure{e}}`; alternatives `\begin{tabular}[b]{@{}l@{}}\inserted{$F$}\\[-.65ex]\begin{tikzpicture}[x=1em,y=1em,baseline=0pt,line width=.45pt]\draw (-.1,-.4)--(.8,.7);\end{tikzpicture}\end{tabular} d\deleted{\unsure{e}}`. The d stem and bowl are not cancelled; the next cell is crossed and admits e. F stands above a short oblique attachment stroke. Narrow doubt to that cancelled e and draw the leader.
- L4 cochain lower relation: `C^n(F)=\coprod_{x\unsure{\in&#124;:}X_n}F(x)`; alternatives `C^n(F)=\coprod_{x\unsure{\in&#124;:}X_n}F(x)`. The coproduct has its open U shape. The small subscript separator breaks into several marks and admits membership or a colon; retain membership first. The two closing parentheses and colon are already outside the box formula in the page.
- L6 s/t overprint: `coéfficien\unsure{\deleted{s}t&#124;\deleted{t}s}\illegible{1}`; alternatives `coéfficien\unsure{\deleted{s}t&#124;\deleted{t}s}\illegible{1}`. The terminal cell contains an s curve and the ascender/crossbar of t; their order is not securely visible. Keep t over s first, with the reverse order explicit. A further clipped cell remains at the edge.
- L7 é/r overprint: `d'interp\unsure{\deleted{é}r&#124;\deleted{r}é}étation`; alternatives `d'interp\unsure{\deleted{é}r&#124;\deleted{r}é}étation`. The crowded cell contains an acute/e trace and an r shoulder, but the printing order is uncertain. The following é is a separate cell. Make the two complete layer orders explicit.
- L9 second variance word: `\unsure{contravariant&#124;contravatiant}`; alternatives `\unsure{contravariant&#124;contravatiant}`. The r/t-like middle cell is crowded and doubled. contravariant remains first, but contravatiant is a possible literal layer reading; the crop does not separate the overtyping.
- L20 cancelled accent: `\deleted{syst\unsure{è&#124;ê}}`; alternatives `\deleted{syst\unsure{è&#124;ê}}`. The cancelled last vowel has a high accent entangled with cancellation ink. Grave and circumflex remain possible; preserve the grave first and localize the doubt to this vowel.
- L22 cancelled expression and strike extent: `\deleted{$\unsure{X&#124;F}$ (resp. $\unsure{X&#124;X'}$)} \unsure{\deleted{soi}t&#124;soit}`; alternatives `\deleted{$\unsure{X&#124;F}$ (resp. $\unsure{X&#124;X'}$)} \unsure{\deleted{soi}t&#124;soit}`. The cancellation still leaves the parenthesis, r/e/s/p and its stop, then an X-like letter. The initial capital admits X/F and the inner prime is uncertain. The pen continues through much of soi, while the final t is uncrossed; mark the strike extent rather than silently deleting the entire word soit.
- L24 cancelled X/U prime: `\deleted{\unsure{X'&#124;U'}}`; alternatives `\deleted{\unsure{X'&#124;U'}}`. A prime is distinct, but the cancelled capital has crossing diagonal and lower-return strokes that admit X or U. X remains preferred; restore a local doubt rather than claim certainty.
- L24 accent below recouvrement: `\deleted{rev\unsure{ê}tement} \inserted{recouvrement}`; alternatives `\deleted{rev\unsure{ê}tement} \inserted{recouvrement}`. The masked word preserves r/e/v, t, e, m and ent beneath recouvrement. The accent on the earlier vowel is obscured by the inserted line; narrow the broad doubt to ê rather than erase it entirely.
- Bottom note, sur/par: `(mais bien \unsure{sur&#124;par}`; alternatives `(mais bien \unsure{sur&#124;par}`. The final handwritten preposition has a long s/p-like entry and a compressed u/a region. sur is favored by the visible open strokes, but par remains possible; retain the uncertainty.

### Unresolved places

- Cancelled parenthetical fragment between peut être and doit: `il`; alternatives `l`. Only an upright/l fragment and incomplete possible i remain beneath x.
- Cancelled raised cette propriété doit: `insertion within the parenthetical revision, placed after peut être`; alternatives `attachment after or over the following short fragment`. Words are clear but no unambiguous leader fixes the exact cancelled insertion point.
- First Ext superscript: `i`; alternatives `insufficient complete stem for a definitive reading`. Right-edge clipping leaves dot and short disconnected serif fragments; R00103 undecided.
- Cancelled word after des in functor line: `fai\unsure{sce}a\unsure{ux}`; alternatives `individual masked cells not fully recoverable`. fai and a have exposed identifying strokes; remaining curved fragments and final x are partly indistinguishable from x masks.
- Cancelled tail after H^i in first item: `pour i m`; alternatives `final m and any intervening original relation are not fully recoverable`. x masks leave an m-like terminal cell but do not expose a secure relation sign.
- Right edge after (do: `n`; alternatives `only clipped upright/arch fragment is secure`. Fragment survives, but rest of n and any following c are absent from scan.
- Right edge after commutatif: `s`; alternatives `only clipped curved/upright fragments are secure`. Remaining part of letter and any punctuation are off the scanned edge.
- Cancelled fragment above last paragraph: `dan`; alternatives `last letters partly masked; no completion to dans`. Cancellation x masks and live line below overlap the fragment.
- Raised cancelled prefix: `\deleted{foncteur \unsure{soit équiv.&#124;plein fid} (resp}`; alternatives `\deleted{foncteur \unsure{soit équiv.&#124;plein fid} (resp}`. After foncteur, the crowded raised line has s/o/i/t-like remnants and an accented following cell. soit équiv. is a possible reading favored by this crop, but the overlapping baseline and x masks prevent a secure recovery; preserve plein fid as the alternative rather than expand either phrase.
- Cancelled mis/mise ending: `\deleted{être \unsure{mise&#124;mis} dans les}`; alternatives `\deleted{être \unsure{mise&#124;mis} dans les}`. Between mis and dans, a curved e-like remnant shares the final cancellation cell. It may be the end of mise, but the mask also permits mis followed by an empty cancelled cell. Mark that narrow word-ending uncertainty.
- First cell of homalement: `\unsure{\deleted{l}h&#124;\deleted{h}l}omalement`; alternatives `\unsure{\deleted{l}h&#124;\deleted{h}l}omalement`. The first cell superposes l/h components without a secure printing order. The third cell is visibly m, not the c needed for localement. Preserve homalement and explicitly allow the reverse l/h layer order.
- Mark after est in first item: `\unsure{!)&#124;\deleted{'}}`; alternatives `\unsure{!)&#124;\deleted{'}}`. The small group has an upper short tick, a dot below it and a separate right-curving stroke. These admit !) as punctuation; the older cancelled-apostrophe interpretation is not conclusively excluded. Preserve both complete readings rather than assert cancellation.
- Third item cohomology exponent: `H^{\unsure{i&#124;1}}`; alternatives `H^{\unsure{i&#124;1}}`. The raised glyph has a short left head below a darker upper projection, consistent with an i whose dot has merged into the stem. A 1 cannot be excluded in this copy; retain i first and mark the local ambiguity.

### Unresolved places

- L2 internal spacing: `\unsure{catégorie&#124;c atégorie}`; alternatives `a full separate space after c`. The c–a advance is wider than neighboring gaps, but no full blank cell is securely established.
- L7 category name: `\unsure{nupcat&#124;n-upcat}`; alternatives `independent hyphen in the crowded n/u interval`. The horizontal stroke may be a separate hyphen or part of the overlapping n/u shapes.
- L9 old upper round character: `\deleted{\unsure{o}}`; alternatives `keyed 0-like round character`. The old raised round form is heavily blotted. Its round-letter notation follows visible cleaner examples, but this instance itself remains uncertain.
- L10 membership C upper form: `\unsure{C^o_0}`; alternatives `C^{\deleted{o}}_0`. The upper internal diagonal may cross the round character or be part of a pen return; no uniform deletion is imposed.
- L14 arrow target: `\unsure{C^o_0}`; alternatives `C^{\deleted{o}}_0`. A tiny dark trace lies inside the upper circle, without a clearly separate full crossing stroke.
- L15 final C: `\unsure{C^{\deleted{1}}_1}`; alternatives `C^1_1`. The curved stroke across the upper 1 is less distinct than the pen cancellation on the other upper 1s; the whole resulting expression remains doubtful.
- L23 cancelled prefix: `\deleted{\unsure{ou&#124;au}}`; alternatives `au`. The first x-masked cell is round but does not securely distinguish o from a.
- L24 blotted old cell: `\deleted{\illegible{1}}`; alternatives `C's old e`. The old cell is physically filled by the blot; no internal e structure can be read with confidence.
- L3 cancelled cell: `d\deleted{\unsure{a&#124;e}}'ensemble`; alternatives `d\deleted{\unsure{a&#124;e}}'ensemble`. The cancelled cell has a lower bowl and right stem suggesting a; its central crossing also admits e beneath the x mask. Preserve both, with a first on this crop.
- L4 overprint in Un: `\unsure{\deleted{q}U&#124;\deleted{U}q}n`; alternatives `\unsure{\deleted{q}U&#124;\deleted{U}q}n`. The superposed capital U and lower q bowl/descender are clear; the order of their typing is not. Preserve both whole layer orders, followed by the separate live n.
- L11 parenthesis/hyphen cell: `celles\unsure{\deleted{(}-&#124;\deleted{-}(}ci`; alternatives `celles\unsure{\deleted{(}-&#124;\deleted{-}(}ci`. One occupied cell has both a full curved parenthesis and a horizontal hyphen. Their order is not independently visible. Localize the uncertainty to that cell and state both complete layer orders.
- L13 insertion terminal curve: `\inserted{x,y\unsure{)}}`; alternatives `\inserted{x,y\unsure{)}}`. The x,y is plain. A thick terminal curve resembles a closing parenthesis but meets the diagonal leader, so the lexical ) remains doubtful. Add the rising attachment stroke and its three detached upper-right fragments; do not discard the possible punctuation merely because it joins the leader.
- L13 Hom overprint: `\underline{\unsure{\deleted{P}H&#124;\deleted{R}H&#124;\deleted{H}P&#124;\deleted{H}R}\mathrm{om}}(x,y)`; alternatives `\underline{\unsure{\deleted{P}H&#124;\deleted{R}H&#124;\deleted{H}P&#124;\deleted{H}R}\mathrm{om}}(x,y)`. The first letter of this Hom contains an extra closed upper bowl on its left upright in addition to the two H uprights. It supports a P-like lower layer, with R possible where the lower strokes merge. The typing order is uncertain; encode the complete alternatives, with H standing first as favored by the repeated notation.
- L20 composite middle cells: `bemis\unsure{\deleted{s}i&#124;\deleted{i}s}\unsure{\deleted{i}m&#124;\deleted{m}i}plicial`; alternatives `bemis\unsure{\deleted{s}i&#124;\deleted{i}s}\unsure{\deleted{i}m&#124;\deleted{m}i}plicial`. ### Unresolved places

- Top RHom subscripts: `x&#124;\bullet`; alternatives `small crossing mark or dot`. Dense lower marks do not resolve shape.
- Cancelled upper line-end word: `se défini\unsure{s&#124;d}`; alternatives `s/d in masked final cell`. Typed x masks the terminal cell.
- Two cells of satisfont: `\deleted{s}i\deleted{i}s`; alternatives `\deleted{i}s\deleted{s}i`. Order of overprinted i/s not recoverable.
- Edge after group: `e`; alternatives `unidentified partial letter`. Only narrow fragment survives.
- Upper marginal opening: `Objection déjà`; alternatives `unresolved ending of first word`. Long cancellation passes through it.
- Upper marginal third line: `résultats`; alternatives `unresolved connected group`. Vertical strokes cover joins.
- Upper marginal remainder: `\illegible{2 lignes}`; alternatives `C's Picard not confirmed`. Two lines are masked and clipped.
- Live marginal opening: `Mais; si on part`; alternatives `partly unreadable beginnings`. Pale/clipped groups.
- Live margin abbreviation: `commut.`; alternatives `unresolved short ending`. Faint strokes.
- Live margin verb: `ne se réalise`; alternatives `incomplete joins`. Pale cursive overlaps edge.
- Live margin ending: `non comm.`; alternatives `partly unreadable abbreviations`. Faint text above underlines.
- Interlinear first verb: `identifie`; alternatives `simplifie`. Compressed initial group.
- Interlinear right-edge word: `dériv`; alternatives `partial final v/u-like stroke`. Source ends before full word.
- Interlinear verb before pi pair: `étant`; alternatives `sont`. Overlaps typed classification.
- Masked group before classification: `\unsure{l&#124;t}\illegible{5}`; alternatives `underlying six-cell word unresolved`. x masks and handwriting overlap.
- Edge after asso: `c`; alternatives `unidentified curved partial cell`. Only narrow left curve survives.
- Edge after seulemen: `t`; alternatives `unidentified upper fragment`. Stem beyond edge.
- Associativite overprint: `\deleted{t}a`; alternatives `\deleted{a}t`. Both forms visible, order unknown.
- Lowered mark after associativite: `,`; alternatives `unidentified cancelled punctuation`. x mask covers all but lower hook.
- Edge article: `\unsure{u}`; alternatives `unidentified partly clipped cell`. Only a left stem survives; added uncertainty is certainty-only relative to C.
- Edge after q: `q\unsure{u}`; alternatives `unidentified partly clipped cell after clear q`. Only the second cell's left stem survives; added uncertainty is certainty-only relative to C.
- Edge after ce: `r`; alternatives `unidentified left-hand fragments`. Right part of cell clipped.
- Top RHom initial R typography: `\unsure{R&#124;\mathbb{R}}`; alternatives `\unsure{R&#124;\mathbb{R}}`. The initial R has two separated upright strokes; they may be a doubled stem rather than a distinct alphabet, so mark that local typography. Both subscripts retain angled rather than plainly round remnants, and x/bullet remains unresolved. After the closing parenthesis, a separate dot above a descending stroke makes a semicolon.
- Interlinear quoted name: `\unsure{Gros&#124;Grab}`; alternatives `\unsure{Gros&#124;Grab}`. A wider audit crop, 162-5_scan009_second_audit_insert_initial.jpg (130,1170,795,1277), restores the left edge of the initial word: its compressed joins still favor identifie over simplifie, without secure resolution. The quoted name has a round o-like middle and final s-like bend without a clear b ascender, so reverse the alternatives to Gros&#124;Grab. cela retains its open c and tall l.
- Body line 19, da/de: `d\unsure{a&#124;e}`; alternatives `d\unsure{a&#124;e}`. The second cell after d has a lower bowl and right stem favoring a, but its dark upper portion also admits e or a composite. Mark the local a/e uncertainty rather than silently normalize to de.
- Small bottom cancelled note: `\deleted{\uuline{\unsure{pourquoi}}}`; alternatives `\deleted{\uuline{\unsure{pourquoi}}}`. Below the cancelled word are two distinct underlining levels, the lower one broken into separated portions. Add double underlining to the doubtful word while retaining its cancellation.

### Unresolved places

- above own page number: `detached shallow arch`; alternatives `purpose or status unknown`. The visible stroke has no secure lexical function; it is reproduced graphically.
- Une following NB: `U\unsure{\deleted{n}n}e`; alternatives `lower p-like component or displaced same-letter impression`. The middle cell has doubled vertically displaced lower strokes.
- first dimension bound: `\handwritten{>}\unsure{\deleted{2}\handwritten{1}}`; alternatives `different old numeral or reinforced 1`. The standing 1 is clear but the old curve is incomplete.
- underlined word at right edge of line 11: `homotopi\unsure{q&#124;c}`; alternatives `clipped c`. The final bowl is cut before a distinguishing right stem or descender.
- marginal abbreviation before fidèle: `\unsure{pl.}`; alternatives `other short abbreviation`. Only low faint strokes and a point survive.
- fourth line of marginal reply: `\unsure{mais} \unsure{pas} \unsure{ess.}\textsuperscript{t}`; alternatives `individual letters in the first two words and short abbreviation remain uncertain`. The strokes are very faint and compressed; the raised t is clearer than the low abbreviation.
- mark to right of upper-right diagram zero: `^{\unsure{\cdot}}`; alternatives `detached punctuation or non-lexical ink mark`. A small dark point is clear, but its intended function is not.
- first quoted Grab: `G\unsure{\deleted{n}r&#124;\deleted{r}n}ab`; alternatives `G\unsure{\deleted{n}r&#124;\deleted{r}n}ab`. The second cell has both the n arch and the r shoulder. Both are visible, but monochrome overtyping does not establish their order; r is the preferred live letter from the repeated designation.
- cancelled cell in first définit: `\deleted{\unsure{i&#124;k}}`; alternatives `\deleted{\unsure{i&#124;k}}`. Une contains two vertically displaced n impressions. The cancelled cell after défi has a narrow upright/foot with crossing ink; i is plausible, k cannot be excluded. It is not fully resolved.
- later Grab-catégorie: `G\unsure{\deleted{R}r&#124;\deleted{r}R}ab`; alternatives `G\unsure{\deleted{R}r&#124;\deleted{r}R}ab`. The cell after G contains an upper R bowl/leg and a lower r shoulder; their order is unresolved. The raised x is separate from the single lower comma, with no second punctuation dot at mid-height.
- overtyped C in 2-Cat: `(2-\unsure{\deleted{C}C&#124;\deleted{o}C&#124;\deleted{C}o}at)`; alternatives `(2-\unsure{\deleted{C}C&#124;\deleted{o}C&#124;\deleted{C}o}at)`. The upper open C is clear, while the lower displaced rounded impression could be C or o. The dark shared right side does not securely settle that lower letter or, if it is o, the overtyping order. C remains the preferred live designation.
- cancelled word after on trouve que: `\deleted{c\unsure{e}tt\unsure{e} c\unsure{a}}`; alternatives `\deleted{c\unsure{e}tt\unsure{e} c\unsure{a}}`. After the five cells of cette, one x occupies the space, then a clear open-right c and a partially masked a-like bowl remain. Recover the last two letter positions without resolving the masked e cells or the final a completely.
- first cell of square range: `\unsure{\deleted{D}0&#124;\deleted{0}D}\handwritten{\leq}i,j\handwritten{\leq}1.`; alternatives `\unsure{\deleted{D}0&#124;\deleted{0}D}\handwritten{\leq}i,j\handwritten{\leq}1.`. The first cell has a seriffed D-like left upright and outer bowl together with the narrower rounded zero impression. A plain 1 does not explain the outer bowl. Both D/0 layer orders remain possible in the monochrome overtype; 0 is preferred for the bound.
- small added diagram prefix and underscript: `\underset{\unsure{\pi_{\unsure{2}}&#124;p.}}{\handwritten{\unsure{C&#124;\alpha&#124;0}}C}_{1o}`; alternatives `\underset{\unsure{\pi_{\unsure{2}}&#124;p.}}{\handwritten{\unsure{C&#124;\alpha&#124;0}}C}_{1o}`. The added prefix is an open/near-closed loop touching the printed C: C, alpha and zero cannot be separated securely. The mark below has two downward legs joined at the top, favouring pi, and a small lower-right triangular mark that may be 2; the earlier p-plus-point remains an alternative. No mathematical completion is imposed.

- L24 handwritten M cell: `\unsure{\deleted{\illegible{1}}\handwritten{M}}ais`; alternatives `Small residual stroke may be part of M rather than a separate old character.`. - L1 length word, third occupied cell: `lo\unsure{\deleted{g}n&#124;\deleted{n}g}gueur`; alternatives `lo\unsure{\deleted{g}n&#124;\deleted{n}g}gueur`. The third cell contains a g descender and an n-shaped shoulder. A reading loggueur alone loses the overlapping n. Both overtyping orders remain, with longueur preferred from the visible shoulder and the repeated term.
- L6 deux correction and two cancelled cells: `deu\unsure{\deleted{t}x&#124;\deleted{x}t}\deleted{\unsure{e}l}`; alternatives `deu\unsure{\deleted{t}x&#124;\deleted{x}t}\deleted{\unsure{e}l}`. The fourth cell has the t ascender and crossbar above the x diagonals; retain both possible orders. In the following masked cells the e is still uncertain, while the last l has an exposed high cap and upright ending in a baseline foot.
- L6 second l-cell of telles: `tel\unsure{\deleted{s}l&#124;\deleted{l}s}es`; alternatives `tel\unsure{\deleted{s}l&#124;\deleted{l}s}es`. The extra tall l overlays a smaller sinuous form with the upper-right and lower-left ends of s. The scan does not settle which was typed last. The lower form is not securely a, and neither impression should be dropped.
- L9 first i-cell of expliciter: `expl\unsure{\deleted{o}i&#124;\deleted{i}o}citer`; alternatives `expl\unsure{\deleted{o}i&#124;\deleted{i}o}citer`. A closed o bowl and the dotted i upright share the cell. Both are distinct, but chronology is not; i remains the preferred live letter. The comma was already included in the full page.
- L16 initial cell of sans: `\unsure{\deleted{(}s&#124;\deleted{s}(}ans`; alternatives `\unsure{\deleted{(}s&#124;\deleted{s}(}ans`. The tall opening-parenthesis contour and the shorter s both survive. Their monochrome overlap cannot establish order, so the composite retains both orders with sans preferred.
- L20 compatible overtype: `compatib\unsure{\deleted{o}l&#124;\deleted{b}l&#124;\deleted{l}o&#124;\deleted{l}b}e`; alternatives `compatib\unsure{\deleted{o}l&#124;\deleted{b}l&#124;\deleted{l}o&#124;\deleted{l}b}e`. There is one shared overtyped cell between the first b and final e, not a separate deleted b followed by a separate l. It contains an l upright/foot and a closed bowl. The bowl favours o but b cannot be excluded because its ascender could coincide with l; the layer order also remains uncertain.
- L20 first cell of tout: `(\unsure{\deleted{d}t&#124;\deleted{t}d}out`; alternatives `(\unsure{\deleted{d}t&#124;\deleted{t}d}out`. The cell after the opening parenthesis has a d ascender/closed bowl plus t crossbar and foot. Both layer orders remain possible; tout is preferred by the visible t and the phrase.
- L22 Un composite cells: `\unsure{\deleted{E}U&#124;\deleted{L}U&#124;\deleted{t}U&#124;\deleted{U}E&#124;\deleted{U}L&#124;\deleted{U}t}\unsure{\deleted{a}n&#124;\deleted{n}a}`; alternatives `\unsure{\deleted{E}U&#124;\deleted{L}U&#124;\deleted{t}U&#124;\deleted{U}E&#124;\deleted{U}L&#124;\deleted{U}t}\unsure{\deleted{a}n&#124;\deleted{n}a}`. The first composite contains a U bowl/right upright plus a displaced left upright and horizontal strokes. E/L remain possible, while the lower cross-stroke also admits t. The second cell contains an n shoulder and an a-like lower bowl/foot. Neither cell establishes chronology; Un remains the preferred live word.

### Unresolved places

- Left margin beside typed lines 2–4: `\uuline{\unsure{oui&#124;sic}}`; alternatives `oui; sic`. The initial open curve and joined final strokes are not uniquely discriminating; double underline is secure.
- Cancelled handwritten line 4 after the colon: `\unsure{esp}`; alternatives `short group not fully recoverable`. Low open strokes and a p-like descender are partly interrupted by cancellation; no dot or longer completion is asserted.
- Typed line 1, final cell of opening word: `qu'i\unsure{\deleted{m}l&#124;\deleted{o}l&#124;\deleted{l}m&#124;\deleted{l}o}`; alternatives `qu'i\unsure{\deleted{m}l&#124;\deleted{o}l&#124;\deleted{l}m&#124;\deleted{l}o}`. The low form has two apparent arches, favouring m, but the l stem can divide a single o bowl. The tall l and a lower impression are secure; neither the lower letter nor the impression order is certain. Keep the possible whole layer orders, with il preferred.
- Raised en général, attachment relative to pas: `\unsure{pas \inserted{en général} l'ensemble&#124;\inserted{en général} pas l'ensemble}`; alternatives `\unsure{pas \inserted{en général} l'ensemble&#124;\inserted{en général} pas l'ensemble}`. The raised words en général span the pas / l’ensemble area and have no insertion leader or keyed caret. Their lettering is clear, but the exact attachment before or after pas is not fixed. Preserve both placements, with after pas first.
- Lower continuation of the rule beside typed lines 10–12: `\begin{tikzpicture}[x=.04cm,y=.04cm,baseline=0pt]\draw[line width=.6pt] (0,25)--(.7,24.9)..controls(1.1,24.7)and(.1,23.0)..(.2,22.0)..controls(.2,21.4)and(1.1,21.0)..(1.1,20.3)--(1.2,17.9);\draw[line width=.6pt] (1.2,17.2)--(1.3,16.3);\draw[line width=.3pt,opacity=.4] (1.35,15.3)--(1.4,15.0);\draw[line width=.3pt,opacity=.4,line cap=round] (1.5,12.7)--(1.5,12.6);\draw[line width=.4pt,line cap=round] (1.8,9.0)--(1.8,8.8);\draw[line width=.55pt] (2.1,6.6)--(2.2,6.2);\draw[line width=.3pt,opacity=.4] (2.5,4.0)--(2.5,3.7);\draw[line width=.6pt,line cap=round] (2.2,.2)--(2.3,.15);\end{tikzpicture}`; alternatives `\begin{tikzpicture}[x=.04cm,y=.04cm,baseline=0pt]\draw[line width=.6pt] (0,25)--(.7,24.9)..controls(1.1,24.7)and(.1,23.0)..(.2,22.0)..controls(.2,21.4)and(1.1,21.0)..(1.1,20.3)--(1.2,17.9);\draw[line width=.6pt] (1.2,17.2)--(1.3,16.3);\draw[line width=.3pt,opacity=.4] (1.35,15.3)--(1.4,15.0);\draw[line width=.3pt,opacity=.4,line cap=round] (1.5,12.7)--(1.5,12.6);\draw[line width=.4pt,line cap=round] (1.8,9.0)--(1.8,8.8);\draw[line width=.55pt] (2.1,6.6)--(2.2,6.2);\draw[line width=.3pt,opacity=.4] (2.5,4.0)--(2.5,3.7);\draw[line width=.6pt,line cap=round] (2.2,.2)--(2.3,.15);\end{tikzpicture}`. The weaker aligned strokes are shown faintly; their precise status remains less secure than the dark rule.

### Resolved marks of witness C

These decisions are recorded separately from changes to the selected source reading. Their original identifiers refer to MARKS.tsv.

| C mark | Decision | Reading | Ink and crop | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| R00174 | first | `compactes?` | The handwriting gives an open c, closed o, two low m humps, descending p, a/c bowls, upright crossed t, and connected e-s ending. A hook plus dot gives the question mark; both parentheses are distinct. A short curved leader attaches after the typed variety adjective. Crop: 162-5_scan013_compactes.jpg [1225, 178, 1464, 275] | high |
| R00176 | first | `\handwritten{2}` | The replacement has a round top, diagonal descent and horizontal foot, all separate from the strikes through the adjacent printed characters. Crop: 162-5_scan013_order_two.jpg [970, 1514, 1311, 1591] | high |
| R00178 | first | `\uline{gradué}` | NB has a separate short underline. The cursive L has a long lower foot and detached apostrophe; anneau has its low joined bowls and humps, followed by est de plus. In gradué the descending g, shoulder r, round a, ascender d and low u-e ending are visible, with a separate acute stroke. Another underline lies under gradué. ### Unresolved places

- main line 1, clipped nécessairem ending: `nécessairem\unsure{e}`; alternatives `another partly cropped round cell`. Only the left fragments of the next cell survive; no completion beyond them is written.
- on pose insertion, last cell: `F^{\cdot}\unsure{(}`; alternatives `unidentified left fragments of a clipped character`. Only two narrow pieces of the left side remain; no argument f or closing brackets survive.
- main line 11, after class: `class\unsure{e}`; alternatives `another cropped round cell`. A left curve and short middle projection survive, without the rest of the cell.
- main line 15, after grou: `grou\unsure{p}`; alternatives `another clipped descender`. The lower foot is below baseline, but the right bowl is missing.
- main line 16, struck inverse phrase; R00175: `\deleted{l'i\unsure{n}verse étant}`; alternatives `masked letter in the n cell`. The cancellation obscures the central shoulder; the l' and other individually legible letters remain outside the doubt.
- main line 16, cancelled word after handwritten numeral; R00177: `\deleted{\unsure{ou}}`; alternatives `other curved letter pair beneath the strike`. Rounded lower bodies survive but the midlines are crossed; the original illegibility mark is not claimed settled.
- main line 19, after tou: `tou\unsure{t}`; alternatives `another cropped crossed stem`. The left crossbar and stem fragments favour t but leave its right side unobserved.
- main line 20, after uti: `uti\unsure{l}`; alternatives `another tall clipped letter`. High serif and low foot survive; most of the connecting stem is lost at the edge.
- First typed line, disputed n/b cell: `différe\unsure{\deleted{b}n&#124;\deleted{n}b&#124;b}tiables`; alternatives `différe\unsure{\deleted{b}n&#124;\deleted{n}b&#124;b}tiables`. The disputed cell has a high b-like stem and bowl, together with a displaced lower shoulder/upright that permits n. A b/n overtype is possible but its order is not secure; a damaged single b remains possible. The reading is no longer asserted as a clean single b.
- Second typed line, two damaged adjective cells: `ori\unsure{e&#124;n&#124;a}\unsure{n&#124;h&#124;b}tables`; alternatives `ori\unsure{e&#124;n&#124;a}\unsure{n&#124;h&#124;b}tables`. The first disputed cell has a closed lower body and irregular upper/middle projection, allowing e/n/a. The next has a high left stroke and shoulder with a heavy bottom join, allowing n with an extraneous high trace or h/b. The expected orientables fits one combination, but the scan does not settle these two damaged cells.
- Universal-property sentence, final joined form: `\unsure{u\unsure{n}&#124;w&#124;u}`; alternatives `\unsure{u\unsure{n}&#124;w&#124;u}`. ### Unresolved places

- L8 first canceled prefix: `\deleted{$\unsure C\,\unsure{:}\,\underline B$}`; alternatives `Initial E rather than C; middle non-colon fragments; no recoverable arrow`. Mask diagonals obscure the first capital and the intervening small fragments. B and its low bar are much clearer than the beginning.
- L8 right clipping: `don\unsure n`; alternatives `don plus unidentifiable partial next letter`. Only the next upright and its feet survive, not its arch/right side.
- L10 canceled prefix: `\deleted{$\unsure{B}($}`; alternatives `E`. Two right bows favor B but share space with strong x diagonals.
- L21 right arrow-label argument: `\unsure q`; alternatives `q with an overprinted underletter; g-like component`. Bowl and right descender favor q, while extra dense left traces prevent full resolution of C's mark.
- L22 remp...çant: `remp\unsure{\deleted l a&#124;\deleted a l}çant`; alternatives `Single h-like appearance; overprinted l/a without established order`. There is one cell, not two, between p and ç. Both the high stem and the low bowl survive.
- L22 upper annotation: `\unsure{(=}\unsure Z_{\unsure o})`; alternatives `Different relation/opening trace; numeral 0 rather than o; incomplete Z-like glyph`. Very few discontinuous strokes survive. The final right parenthesis is clearer and is left outside the doubt.
- L22 annotation attachment: `upper annotation attached after Z`; alternatives `Some lower fragments may include punctuation, but no secure comma`. Small marks descend beside and below Z; continuity is broken, so attachment is recorded separately from lexical content.
- L22 final cell: `cobor\unsure{\deleted a d&#124;\deleted d a}`; alternatives `Plain d with an unusual impression; reverse order of a/d`. A flagged ascender and extra central/low bowl share the cell. The next line independently begins dant.
- Insertion after L23 X: `\unsure e st le \unsure{même}`; alternatives `est le même in full; C's two longer phrases do not match the visible occupied groups`. Initial e is fragmentary and much of the last word is faded. The s/t, l/e group and short accent support the proposed phrase, not certainty.
- L26 canceled tail after T: `\deleted{application T \unsure{une}}`; alternatives `Other u-/n-/e-like letter remnants; masks over spaces`. The lower traces suggest une, but cancellation strokes crowd each letter and no extra hidden letters can be counted securely.
- L26 transversale composite: `tr\unsure{\deleted q a&#124;\deleted a q}nsversale`; alternatives `Either impression order; not two live characters`. The divided a-like bowl and descending q-like limb are both visible. Sense cannot determine which layer stands.
- L11 last clipped cell: `aus\illegible{1}`; alternatives `aus\illegible{1}`. After the complete aus a small dark fragment occupies the next keyed cell at the scan boundary. It does not establish s or any other complete character; record one unreadable clipped cell without supplying si.
- L12 composite final word: `\unsure{\deleted{D}O&#124;\deleted{L}O&#124;\deleted{O}D&#124;\deleted{O}L}\unsure{\deleted{a}n&#124;\deleted{n}a}`; alternatives `\unsure{\deleted{D}O&#124;\deleted{L}O&#124;\deleted{O}D&#124;\deleted{O}L}\unsure{\deleted{a}n&#124;\deleted{n}a}`. The first cell has a rounded O-like contour and an internal upright/foot compatible with either D or L. The second has an a-like bowl and an n-like shoulder. The surface does not establish which layer was typed first, so both orders remain explicit, with On first.
- L19 crowded cell after au: `au\unsure{\deleted{d}-&#124;\deleted{-}d&#124;\deleted{a}d&#124;\deleted{d}a}dessus`; alternatives `au\unsure{\deleted{d}-&#124;\deleted{-}d&#124;\deleted{a}d&#124;\deleted{d}a}dessus`. The crowded cell contains a high d-like trace, a lower bowl and a conspicuous horizontal middle bar. That bar permits hyphen over d as well as the earlier a/d reading; the overtype order is not recoverable. Put au-dessus first without treating the expected spelling as a secure resolution.
- L24 first boundary index: `\mathrm{bord}(T)=Z_{\handwritten{\unsure{o&#124;0}}}\amalg Z_1`; alternatives `\mathrm{bord}(T)=Z_{\handwritten{\unsure{o&#124;0}}}\amalg Z_1`. Below the first Z there are a detached dark point and a short curved upright, too incomplete to separate o from 0. The disjoint-union sign, second index 1 and following closing parenthesis are clear; the parenthesis already follows the display fragment in the page.

### Unresolved places

- L2 object prefixes: `\unsure{t&#124;\dagger}\,\unsure{i}; \unsure{t&#124;\dagger&#124;F}; \unsure{t&#124;\dagger}`; alternatives `t-like or dagger-like remnants; unknown function`. Three structured upright/crossbar groups survive; the first has a small dotted/upright fragment. Neither witness identifies them.
- L2 second arrow: `\alpha_{i\unsure{*}}`; alternatives `alpha_i followed by a broken star or another mark`. Final mark is a small incomplete dark fragment.
- L2 after final comma: `\unsure{'}`; alternatives `apostrophe-like stroke or accidental ink`. Short upright is present but has no secure identity.
- Diagram right label: `\unsure{\Gamma=&#124;\Gamma_{\underline{a}}}\unsure{(}p,\unsure{\mathrm{id}&#124;1}_X)`; alternatives `relation/opening/id fragments may differ`. The relation and short letter group are faint and broken.
- L4 last operation mark: `i_{s\unsure{*}}`; alternatives `another fragmented small lower mark`. There are disconnected star-like bits, insufficient to remove C’s doubt.
- L8 generator tail: `($i$ entier \unsure{$\geq&#124;>$} \illegible{2}`; alternatives `greater/low-bar group followed by unidentified mixed continuation`. The restriction tail is broken and clipped/faint; H’s fuller formula is not visible here.
- L9 H exponent: `H^{\unsure{B}}`; alternatives `B-shaped overwritten material; possible lower dot`. A B outline and lower blot cannot be separated into a secure correction layer.
- en fait H exponent: `H(X)`; alternatives `H with a raised bullet`. No separate bullet is secure; a faint spot near the parenthesis is not enough for H’s exponent.
- L12 cancelled first letter: `\deleted{\unsure{n}ote}`; alternatives `another masked initial cell before ote`. The first cell’s arch is mostly lost under the x mask.
- L14 cancelled middle cell: `\deleted{t\unsure{e}l}`; alternatives `another middle cell between clear t and l`. x mask obscures the inner bowl.
- L24 final cutoff: `u\illegible{1}`; alternatives `u followed by a partial n or other upright letter`. Only the next cell’s left stem/serifs remain; identity is not secure.
- L9 interlinear attachment: `after homomorphisme évident: \inserted{d'anne\unsure{\deleted{d}a&#124;\deleted{a}d}\unsure{\deleted{t}u&#124;\deleted{u}t}xp\unsure{\deleted{d}g&#124;\deleted{g}d&#124;g}\unsure{\deleted{i}r&#124;\deleted{r}i}adués}`; alternatives `insertion point somewhere within or after the phrase`. The raised phrase is above homomorphisme évident without a precise caret.
- Equation, second alpha and final lower star: `\alpha_s^*\unsure{\alpha}_{s*}=\Gamma^*i_{s\unsure{*}}`; alternatives `\alpha_s^*\unsure{\alpha}_{s*}=\Gamma^*i_{s\unsure{*}}`. The second alpha is reduced to three disconnected remnants, so its reading needs a local doubt. The last small star is likewise fragmented and stays doubtful; the first alpha and the two upper stars are clearer. The following comma is already in the page.
- Cancelled letter immediately before degree zero: `\deleted{\unsure{z&#124;s}} 0`; alternatives `\deleted{\unsure{z&#124;s}} 0`. The cancelled cell retains a low diagonal and angular upper/lower remnants favouring z, but the middle is occupied by the x mask and an s-like curve cannot be excluded. Keep the deletion while making the letter doubtful.
- Raised typewriter insertion, crowded a/u/g/r cells: `\inserted{d'anne\unsure{\deleted{d}a&#124;\deleted{a}d}\unsure{\deleted{t}u&#124;\deleted{u}t}xp\unsure{\deleted{d}g&#124;\deleted{g}d&#124;g}\unsure{\deleted{i}r&#124;\deleted{r}i}adués}`; alternatives `\inserted{d'anne\unsure{\deleted{d}a&#124;\deleted{a}d}\unsure{\deleted{t}u&#124;\deleted{u}t}xp\unsure{\deleted{d}g&#124;\deleted{g}d&#124;g}\unsure{\deleted{i}r&#124;\deleted{r}i}adués}`. Several cells are crowded: the a after anne has a high d-like ascender, u has an internal t-like crossing stroke, and r has an additional i-like dot/upright. The g cell may include a d-like underlayer but also permits a damaged single g. The ordering of impressions is not secure, so whole-cell alternatives retain both orders. The separate keyed p before g remains literal.

### Unresolved places

- L4 after gradués, local machine cancellation within the larger cancelled block: `d\deleted{\unsure{e}} d`; alternatives `See locally delimited alternatives in the proposed reading and the evidence row 017-03`. The two outer occupied cells have d ascenders and bowls. Between them one x mask contains a low e-like loop/crossbar; the following mask lies in a spacing cell. Those x strokes are cancellation marks, not literal x letters. The e identification remains uncertain; all of the fragment also lies within the large crossed paragraph.
- L8 long cancelled run after toute X: `\deleted{l\unsure{e&#124;a} \unsure{sous} \unsure{anneau} ensem\unsure{b}}`; alternatives `partial letter recovery only; no completed phrase`. This is a long occupied run extending to the clipped right edge. Initial l-like flag/upright, then e/a-like bowl; a four-cell sous-like sequence is visible under the masks. In the following six cells there are an a-like low loop, two masked shoulders, a round/crossbar e-like form, another low loop and a u-like return; anneau is retained only as a whole-word doubtful candidate. Terminal e, n-like arch, s-like curves, e and m-like arches are followed by a clipped b-like ascender/bowl. The underlying phrase is not completed from grammar.
- L9 before l'ensemble, locally cancelled prefix: `\deleted{\unsure{d&#124;l}}`; alternatives `See locally delimited alternatives in the proposed reading and the evidence row 017-10`. The first masked cell has a flagged ascender and a low left bowl/return, suggesting d; l cannot be excluded because the x mask supplies some of that lower contour. The next occupied x is a cancellation mask and has no separable underlying letter.
- L11 right-edge handwritten addition after S(X): `\handwritten{e\unsure{n&#124;t}}`; alternatives `\handwritten{e\unsure{n&#124;t}}`. The e loop is clear; the following tall, partly clipped stroke can be a damaged n or t. The phrase en termes favours n, but the edge has removed the right-hand evidence needed to exclude t.
- L18 géométrique, the two cells after géo: `géo\unsure{\deleted{é}m&#124;\deleted{m}é}\unsure{\deleted{m}é&#124;\deleted{é}m}trique`; alternatives `See locally delimited alternatives in the proposed reading and the evidence row 017-18`. Both adjacent cells carry separate acute strokes. Their bodies contain m-like vertical/arched feet together with e-like middle bars and round contours. Thus there are two composite m/é cells. Their impression order cannot be distinguished; both proposed orders are retained for each whole cell.
- L19 Pour, detached stroke above the o: `P\unsure{\u{o}}ur`; alternatives `See locally delimited alternatives in the proposed reading and the evidence row 017-21`. A short shallow U-shaped stroke stands above the o, separate from its complete oval. It is physically present but its role as a breve, an earlier impression, or stray correction stroke is uncertain. The proposed accented cell preserves the shape without asserting a normal French accent.
- L19 ceci, final two typed cells: `ce\unsure{\deleted{i}c&#124;\deleted{c}i}\unsure{\deleted{c}i&#124;\deleted{i}c}`; alternatives `See locally delimited alternatives in the proposed reading and the evidence row 017-22`. Each of the last two cells has c-like round contours together with an i upright/foot and a separate high dot. The first is not a plain c and the last is not a plain i. The relative order of the c and i impressions is not recoverable, so uncertainty covers each whole proposed old/standing cell.
- L20 locally cancelled additives sur l: `\deleted{additives sur \unsure{l&#124;i}}`; alternatives `See locally delimited alternatives in the proposed reading and the evidence row 017-24`. The crossing stroke traverses additives, sur, and the last cell. The word bodies are recoverable. The final cell has a separated top fragment, a leftward short flag-like stroke and a lower hooked upright; l is favored, but i remains possible in this worn cell.
- L21 prefix before foncteur: `\deleted{le}`; alternatives `See locally delimited alternatives in the proposed reading and the evidence row 017-25`. The first masked cell retains a high l flag/upright; the second contains an e-like upper loop and low return under the x. Both cells are machine-cancelled; the second x is not a live x character.
- L23 right clipped edge: `So\unsure{n&#124;i}`; alternatives `So\unsure{n&#124;i}`. The final edge cell retains only an upper dark remnant and a lower foot-like point. N is favoured by Son dual, but the surviving fragments do not exclude i. No clipped completion is supplied.
- Interline above L24, first cell after anti: `anti\unsure{o&#124;c}ommutative`; alternatives `o-shaped cell or c-shaped cell; no separate cancelled o asserted`. Only one occupied cell occurs between anti and the ordinary following o. It has a nearly closed/full oval with a slight upper-left break. No independent diagonal x mask, extra c cell, or separable deleted-o impression can be established. The literal o-shaped reading is first; c remains a narrow alternative, but would require treating the right closure as non-letter ink. C's extra cancelled o plus separate c is not supported.
- L26 right clipped edge: `d'aille\unsure{u}`; alternatives `See locally delimited alternatives in the proposed reading and the evidence row 017-34`. After the complete e a further clipped stem has a short upper cap and a low right-turning return, consistent with the left portion of u. That visible partial cell is retained under doubt; r and s are absent. H's urs is supplied restoration.
- Bottom handwritten continuation, trivialement: `tri\unsure{via}le\unsure{men}t`; alternatives `See locally delimited alternatives in the proposed reading and the evidence row 017-37`. The first tall crossed t and following r arch are clear; the i dot and later tall l/low e contour are visible. Several intervening/downstream letters survive only as detached dots or short humps: the via and men runs are retained as local candidates rather than silently certain prose. The final t has a surviving upright.
- Bottom handwritten formula after sur and cancelled item: `$H^{\unsure{\deleted{2}1&#124;\deleted{1}2&#124;q&#124;0}}({\unsure{S&#124;\lambda&#124;\Delta}}^{\unsure{1&#124;r&#124;3}})$`; alternatives `$H^{\unsure{\deleted{2}1&#124;\deleted{1}2&#124;q&#124;0}}({\unsure{S&#124;\lambda&#124;\Delta}}^{\unsure{1&#124;r&#124;3}})$`. The raised H cell has an upright, loop and crossing/baseline remnants allowing the recorded 1/2 overwork, but the blind q/0 readings cannot be excluded. Inside the parentheses only a low wedge-like base remains, allowing S, lambda or Delta; its raised mark can be 1, r or 3. Keep these as explicit formula doubts. The cancelled fragments before and after the formula remain unreadable.
- L27 tensor-like prefix: `\unsure{\otimes&#124;\Omega&#124;Q}`; alternatives `\unsure{\otimes&#124;\Omega&#124;Q}`. The oval/arch has two heavy lower feet and only fragments of a possible internal crossing. Tensor remains the context-compatible primary reading, but the intact outline also permits Omega or a damaged Q. Record those graphic alternatives instead of claiming a complete tensor cross.
- bottom cancelled run before H: `\deleted{\illegible{1}}`; alternatives `no secure word`. Before H there is a crossed handwritten run with several low angular humps, a detached oblique upper stroke, and small lower flecks. The cancellation makes character boundaries physically unreadable; no specific word is securely recovered.
- bottom cancelled run after period: `\deleted{\illegible{1}}`; alternatives `no secure word or stable symbol recovery`. After the clear period a cancelled compact run has rounded outline/loop remnants on the left and several long crossing diagonals plus a low turning stroke on the right. Neither a word nor a stable symbolic identity can be separated confidently; retained illegibility denotes that isolated cancelled run, outside the preceding formula.
- Cancelled (en ve, first vowel: `\deleted{(\unsure{e&#124;o}n ve}`; alternatives `\deleted{(\unsure{e&#124;o}n ve}`. The first oval after the parenthesis has lost its middle bar and part of the right/lower contour. En is favoured by the phrase, but a damaged o cannot be excluded. The right edge stops at ve.
- Cancelled celle, first cell: `\deleted{catégorie dans \unsure{c&#124;o}elle}`; alternatives `\deleted{catégorie dans \unsure{c&#124;o}elle}`. The first cell of celle appears almost closed, unlike the clearer open c in catégorie. It may be a clogged c or an o. Keep the word-compatible c first but record the circular alternative inside the cancellation.
- Clipped tro ending: `\deleted{C'est un peu tr\unsure{o}}`; alternatives `\deleted{C'est un peu tr\unsure{o}}`. The last cell is cut by the paper edge, retaining only the left arc and a short upper segment. O is plausible but cannot be read as a complete oval. No final p is supplied.
- Cancelled en, lower/capital case: `\deleted{\unsure{e&#124;E}n prenant comme}`; alternatives `\deleted{\unsure{e&#124;E}n prenant comme}`. The first letter is covered by both the x mask and the long diagonal cancellation. Its surviving upper and lower strokes do not securely distinguish lowercase e from capital E. Preserve the lowercase primary reading and make the case uncertainty explicit.
- Cancelled ceci, masked e: `\deleted{correspondances Pour c\unsure{e}ci}`; alternatives `\deleted{correspondances Pour c\unsure{e}ci}`. The tall initial P and its upper bowl support Pour, not lowercase pour. After it there are four occupied cells: c, a masked e-like cell, c and dotted i. Keep ceci with only the obscured e doubtful, not the shortened coi.
- Clipped homo ending: `hom\unsure{o}`; alternatives `hom\unsure{o}`. Only the left arc of the last cell survives at the paper edge. Keep homo/gique as written but mark the clipped o rather than asserting a full visible oval.
- Clipped add ending: `ad\unsure{d&#124;c}`; alternatives `ad\unsure{d&#124;c}`. The last edge cell retains a c-shaped lower arc but lacks the right side and upper ascender area. D is favoured by add/tive; c is the remaining visible alternative. Preserve the clipping without completing additive.

### Unresolved places

- L2 cancelled word: `stable`; alternatives `unrecoverable masked letters`. a and l survive, while several letters are hidden by x masks.
- L2 right edge: `l`; alternatives `incomplete terminal stem`. After the complete first l, another high serif and partial stem survive at the source cutoff; the next line visibly begins le.
- L5 cancelled noun: `vectoriels`; alternatives `first and last masked letters`. The masked v/s are not independently secure; C first reading is retained.
- L6 cancellation ending: `est`; alternatives `compressed e/s cells and spacing`. Certainty-only relative to C; t is visible, adjacent cells are obscured.
- L9 article: `\deleted{t}l`; alternatives `\deleted{l}t`. Both upper l-serif and middle t-crossbar survive in one cell.
- L9 right edge: `o`; alternatives `c`. The last cell has a left oval with short upper and lower returns and an open right side. A damaged o fits the clipped word, but the surviving outline also reads c. Keep o first and add c; do not supply the absent final n.
- L11 second cell: `\deleted{i}n`; alternatives `\deleted{n}i`. The dot and inner upright overlap the n-shaped arch.
- L11 right edge: `n`; alternatives `r`. The final cell retains its left upright, upper shoulder and foot but lacks a secure right leg at the sheet edge. N is favoured by the phrase; the r-like surviving shape is also possible.
- L14 word middle: `nit`; alternatives `inseparable superposed cluster`. Certainty-only: close n/i/t uprights and a crossing bar overlap. No distinct cancelled underletter or layer order can be established safely.
- M1, first slanted line: `ui, car son`; alternatives `unreadable or clipped portions`. Two low humps and a dotted i survive at the left edge; no separate initial o is recoverable. The open c, reduced ar and son follow.
- M2, second slanted line: `nneau affine`; alternatives `unreadable or clipped portions`. The first surviving edge stroke joins the n arches; the initial a is beyond the cutoff. Affine has paired tall f strokes and a dotted i.
- M3, third slanted line: `\unsure{t} une \unsure{algèbre}`; alternatives `unreadable or clipped portions`. An edge remnant is compatible with terminal t; une has three low joins. The following ascender, g-descender and small looped strokes favor algèbre; no separate de is secure here.
- M4, fourth slanted line: `\unsure{y}nômes à une`; alternatives `unreadable or clipped portions`. The left edge cuts the word; a descending fork, low n/m chain and detached circumflex survive, followed by à une. Lost initial letters are not supplied.
- M5, fifth slanted line: `\unsure{f}inité d'indéterminées,`; alternatives `unreadable or clipped portions`. A clipped tall/descending initial stroke and the remaining dotted uprights favor finité; the d-loop, apostrophe, t ascender and accented ending of the next word are visible.
- M6, cancelled fragment and continuation: `\deleted{\illegible{1}} \unsure{réunion d'algèbres de}`; alternatives `unreadable or clipped portions`. One residual word group is crossed by two heavy strokes; no letters are secure. Following low joins favor réunion; further ascenders/loops cross the typed groupe and remain uncertain.
- M7, seventh slanted line: `\unsure{polynômes} à \unsure{augmentation}`; alternatives `compressed middle letters`. The rotated view retains ui, car son; the clipped nneau affine; the algèbre-like ascender and g-descender; the circumflex and low n/m joins of ynômes; and the long indéterminées group. The lower line after à has an initial oval/u join, a clear g-like descender, low middle joins and a tall terminal group supporting augmentation, though the middle remains compressed. Recover that word with a doubt. The cancelled fragment and réunion d’algèbres de remain unresolved under crossed strokes and typed overlap. The last sign is a closed triangle, not an arrow followed by zero.
- M8, final slanted line: `\unsure{is} stables par $\Delta$`; alternatives `unreadable or clipped portions`. Only a small edge stroke, dotted upright and return remain before stables. Stables has t/b/l ascenders; par ends beside a closed three-sided capital delta.
- L21 cancelled capital: `T`; alternatives `Y`. x masks obscure the lower capital structure; flat upper remnants favor T.
- L21 final target: `H`; alternatives `F`. The tangent formula has X beneath its first T, Y beneath the second, a minus and raised dot. The following map has a horizontal shaft and only an upper terminal barb. The cut target retains a left stem and two horizontal remnants, compatible with H or F. Use the upper harpoon in ordinary mathematics and preserve both target readings.
- L23 second cell: `\deleted{u}o`; alternatives `\deleted{o}u`. The oval supports o and extra serif/stem remnants support u; chronology remains open.
- L24 worn accented cell: `é`; alternatives `body not fully recoverable`. Certainty-only: the hand acute is clear but the keyed body is reduced to small left remnants.
- H1 parenthetic word: `disons`; alternatives `compressed middle letters`. The d ascender and s-like end survive; central joined letters remain reduced.
- H3 group after M: `H`; alternatives `looped or corrected group`. Certainty-only: two tall stems and a cross-stroke favor H but extra loops obscure its form.
- H3 reduced word after stables d’: `stables d'\unsure{espaces}`; alternatives `stables d'\unsure{espaces}`. The tall t/b/l stems establish stables. After d’, the low initial joins, long p-like descender and ending wave favour espaces, but the individual middle letters are too compressed to support the former unqualified resolution. Restore a localized word doubt without supplying the blind abbreviation.

### Resolved marks of witness C

These decisions are recorded separately from changes to the selected source reading. Their original identifiers refer to MARKS.tsv.

| C mark | Decision | Reading | Ink and crop | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| R00237 | first | `pas` | After rotation, the first line begins n and a clear raised apostrophe, followed by intervi and a low en run. The tall late stroke and short remainder favor dra but remain faint and cross the vertical rule. The second line has the long p descender and low as run, then plus bas. Each line has its own underline; the adjoining long rule is not a cancellation. Crop: 162-5_scan022_margin_note.jpg [24,1673,251,1949] | high |

### Unresolved places

- Opening cancelled prefix: `i\unsure{n}d\unsure{-}`; alternatives `ind-; the middle letter or trailing sign may remain unrecoverable`. The strike masks the middle hump and any independent hyphen.
- Low glyph after et: `\unsure{s&#124;d}`; alternatives `s-like or d-like character; state and attachment uncertain`. Small hooked dark form; no clear separate apostrophe or cancelling stroke.
- Cancelled sign after coproduct: `\deleted{\unsure{\longrightarrow}}`; alternatives `right-pointing arrow; left end and full sign form masked`. Right point remains visible; cancellation hatching covers the left portion.
- Article before cancelled catégorie: `\uline{u}\unsure{n&#124;ne}`; alternatives `un; une`. Low final humps do not separate n and ne confidently; short underline lies below u.
- After rôle: `d'\unsure{un&#124;u}`; alternatives `d'un; d'u`. Apostrophe is visible, but the final n cannot be isolated from the low multi-hump run.
- Second projection superscript in displayed df: `\unsure{\alpha&#124;*&#124;d}`; alternatives `alpha; malformed star; d`. The raised sign over the lower 1 has a loop and an ascending branch. That fits the retained alpha-like reading and can also resemble d; a malformed star remains possible in view of the adjoining projection. Add d without normalizing to the mathematically expected star.
- First marginal line ending: `n'intervien\unsure{dra&#124;t}`; alternatives `n'interviendra; n'intervient`. Late ascender and compressed final strokes are faint and cross the adjoining rule.
- Projection superscript in lower inline map: `f\longmapsto\mathrm{pr}_{\unsure{2&#124;\alpha}}^{\unsure{*}}(f):\underline O_{\unsure{X_0&#124;X}}\mathbin{-}A_\alpha`; alternatives `lower 2/alpha; upper star; ring X0/X`. The projection’s small lower loop can be 2 or alpha. At the end of the ring subscript X is a tiny filled circular return, compatible with a lower 0 but also with the returning pen stroke of X. Prefer X0 with X retained. The map from f has a left bar and full right head; the later horizontal stroke between the ring and A has no head, so it remains a minus. The raised star remains locally doubtful.
- Middle of cancelled lower phrase: `\deleted{f\unsure{orme} li}`; alternatives `forme li favored; middle cells physically obscured`. The f, l and dotted i survive outside the cancelling line, while the low middle cells merge with it.
- Lower index of first D after Alors: `\unsure{0&#124;\mathcal C&#124;\mathrm g}`; alternatives `0; category C; g`. The first D has a lower closed loop with a short pointed return. It favours 0, but a curled C or g can fit; add g to the retained alternatives. The line under lim is plain, without an arrowhead, and alpha is beneath it. Keep underlined lim, not a directed-limit arrow.
- Added qualifier after unitaires: `unitaires \unsure{nc.&#124;m.&#124;n.}`; alternatives `nc.; m.; n.`. Unitaires is clear. Its short qualifier has two compressed arches and a final stop; nc., m. or n. fit better than the formerly retained ass. The broad leader has a shallow left span, deep central attachment loop and rising hooked right end. Add that continuous path. The blind extra short horizontal stroke at the right belongs to the following square bracket, so it is not duplicated as a drawing.
- Heading, angular i mark: `tap\unsure{i&#124;\^{\i}}s`; alternatives `tap\unsure{i&#124;\^{\i}}s`. The detached mark over the i is angular, with two tiny inclined sides. It may be an angular dot or a circumflex. Keep the ordinary word tapis first, with the accent shape unresolved; Quillen and all date digits are clear.
- Article before objet: `l\unsure{e&#124;es} 'objet`; alternatives `l\unsure{e&#124;es} 'objet`. The tall l is followed by a low filled e and a small terminal curl. The curl could contain an abbreviated s, so le remains preferred but les cannot be excluded. The raised apostrophe and separate o are plain.
- Initial joins of regardé: `\unsure{re}gardé`; alternatives `\unsure{re}gardé`. The initial re joins are faint and compressed before the clear g descender, ard sequence and accented e. Localize the uncertainty to re rather than treating the whole word as secure.
- Final form of underlined ind-schéma: `ind-schém\unsure{a&#124;.}`; alternatives `ind-schém\unsure{a&#124;.}`. The end after m is a small rounded closed mark that can be a reduced a or a stop. The expanded word is favoured by its connected position, but a terminal abbreviation point remains possible. Keep the preceding i.e. and following particulier from the wider reading.
- First capital in coproduct: `\unsure{A}`; alternatives `\unsure{A}`. The first capital has a dark triangular A shape with faint extra looped ink that prevents a wholly secure letter reading. The live arrow has a full head and a Delta label; the tensor, right/left module indices and underlined ring are clear. The last cancelled sign retains a right point but its left structure is masked. Keep both arrows as mathematical signs, not pictures.
- First vis à vis phrase: `\unsure{vis&#124;vs} à \unsure{vis&#124;vs}`; alternatives `\unsure{vis&#124;vs} à \unsure{vis&#124;vs}`. Each vis-like word has two compact joined groups, with no separately secure dotted i. Vis fits the low joins and the phrase, but the shortened vs reading cannot be excluded. Preserve both occurrences as local uncertainties.
- Prefix of pro-faisceau: `\unsure{pro-&#124;p-}faisceau`; alternatives `\unsure{pro-&#124;p-}faisceau`. The prefix has a clear p descender and a horizontal join before f. The intervening r/o shapes are compressed into that join; pro- is favoured, while the abbreviated p- shape is also possible.
- Both Hom ring subscripts: `\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\underline O_{\unsure{X_0&#124;X}}}(A_\alpha,\underline O_{\unsure{X_0&#124;X}})=\underline{\mathscr D}_\alpha^{\mathcal C}`; alternatives `\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\underline O_{\unsure{X_0&#124;X}}}(A_\alpha,\underline O_{\unsure{X_0&#124;X}})=\underline{\mathscr D}_\alpha^{\mathcal C}`. Both ring subscripts have crossed X strokes followed by a tiny upturned closed or filled return. The return may be a separate 0 but is not cleanly separable from X, so retain X0/X at both places. Hom and D each have their underline. The two-loop D form follows the established script-D alphabet; alpha and upper category C are distinct.
- Ring after différentiel dans: `\underline O_{\unsure{X_0&#124;X}}`; alternatives `\underline O_{\unsure{X_0&#124;X}}`. The ring after dans has an underlined O and crossed X followed by a small round returning stroke. As in the two independently inspected Hom subscripts, this can be a small 0 or the terminal return of X; keep X0/X. The final stop is separate.
- Bracketed justification after unitaires: `\unsure{grâce à&#124;gr. à :}`; alternatives `\unsure{grâce à&#124;gr. à :}`. After the initial g descender and r, the low compressed joins and small raised mark can be read grâce followed by à. The same compact ink could instead be the written abbreviation gr. à and a colon. Keep the whole phrase alternatives, without silently expanding the possible abbreviation. The bracket, application and Delta are clear.
