### Unresolved places

- L3, cancelled word after first equality: `\deleted{\unsure{\mathrm{dual}&#124;\text{déf}&#124;\text{dér}}}`; alternatives `dual; déf; dér`. The initial Gothic letter has the returned lower descender of g and its upper C is clear. In the cancelled word, d and a final tall arch survive but the middle is hidden; dual remains preferred, while déf and dér cannot be excluded. The first Omega’s upper C-like form is crossed by dense cancelling ink, not an unqualified live e or ell. Its lower C and the Hom ring X0 are visible. The target ring has X without a separate lower 0; do not regularize it.
- L3, cancelled upper character on first Omega: `\deleted{\unsure{\mathcal C}}`; alternatives `C-like raised character; no secure second candidate`. Outer lower return survives, interior opaque.
- L6, cancelled raised character after star: `\deleted{\unsure{\mathcal C}}`; alternatives `C-like raised character; no secure second candidate`. Dense strike fills most of the small loop.
- L9, cancelled word before Module: `\deleted{sch\unsure{é}}`; alternatives `Last low letter é tentative; no longer completion established`. s/c/h remnants survive; last low loop is obscured by the mask.
- L9, word before X_0: `sché\unsure{m&#124;ma}.`; alternatives `schém. first; schéma. alternative`. Final flat run favors m but can hide ma; the separate point is secure.
- L10, sign or abbreviation between de and que: `\unsure{\overline{\mathrm m}&#124;=}`; alternatives `overlined m first; uneven equality-like mark alternative`. Upper stroke straight, lower stroke has a hollow/hump and long tail.
- L11, cancelled fragment after une: `\deleted{\unsure{o}p}`; alternatives `o tentative; p secure`. First round head is crossed; second long looped descender remains exposed.
- L9, first inserted abbreviation: `\unsure{qu.&#124;q.} coh`; alternatives `\unsure{qu.&#124;q.} coh`. The first abbreviation has a clear q descender and a long flattened right return that can contain u or be q’s exit stroke; keep qu./q. Both the stop after it and coh without a final stop are visible. The insertion leader rises from after Module, crosses the space before Omega and ends in a small upper return; refine that final hook while leaving all letters outside the drawing.
- L16, added law label: `\unsure{\alpha&#124;\alpha'})`; alternatives `\unsure{\alpha&#124;\alpha'})`. The alpha label has a small raised return immediately before its parenthesis that might be a separate prime or the final stroke of alpha. Keep unprimed alpha first and retain the prime alternative. The delta-zero indices, fg product, plus and final g agree.
- L19, compatibility label: `\unsure{\beta'&#124;\beta})`; alternatives `\unsure{\beta'&#124;\beta})`. A separate short dark raised mark lies between beta and the closing parenthesis, in a prime position. Prefer beta-prime but retain beta if the mark is incidental ink. Every delta index is separately visible: the first right-hand term contains delta0(f) followed by delta1(omega), even though those degrees do not match the left side. Preserve that literal formula.

### Unresolved places

- opening old prefix: `h\unsure{omo&#124;om}`; alternatives `h\unsure{omo&#124;om}`. The initial cancelled h has an exposed ascender and shoulder. The following round bodies are obscured, with homo preferred but the final o not separable from the strike; retain omo/om. Above it, anti is clear. Its reinforced underline turns downward at the right into a distinct hook. Draw that connected underline/attachment, with anti as ordinary text.
- top insertion opening: `\unsure{grâce}`; alternatives `compressed letter bodies not separately secure`. descending initial and flat ending with high accent
- top cancelled phrase: `à la premi\unsure{ère}`; alternatives `masked ending`. last low letters/upper mark intersect strike
- old top reference: `\unsure{\beta)}`; alternatives `unresolved old sign`. replacement and cancellation intersect double-loop body
- new first upper reference: `\unsure{\alpha)&#124;\alpha/}`; alternatives `\unsure{\alpha)&#124;\alpha/}`. The first upper sign has an alpha-like loop and crossing, followed by an oblique terminal that can be a compressed closing parenthesis or slash. The following beta and right parenthesis are distinct. Retain alpha)/alpha/ as whole local alternatives; this shared insertion was applied with B004.
- heading short word: `\unsure{\deleted{de}&#124;de}`; alternatives `live de versus cancelled de`. thin extension may be strike or word entry
- heading edge: `Rha\unsure{m}`; alternatives `incomplete final letter`. native right edge clips low tail
- old preposition: `\unsure{sur}`; alternatives `not securely recoverable`. middle letters under dark mask
- old pairing argument: `\unsure{(\omega)}`; alternatives `obscured boundary/central sign`. new closing angle intersects old writing
- first sign layers: `\unsure{\deleted{-}+&#124;+&#124;\deleted{+}-}`; alternatives `\unsure{\deleted{-}+&#124;+&#124;\deleted{+}-}`. The same two dark points precede delta-omega. At the following sign, a tall plus stem crosses a dense horizontal patch; minus changed to plus is preferred, but plain plus and reversed cancellation order cannot be excluded. State whole alternatives rather than asserting an impression chronology. The separate later minus is above the cancelled plus, as already transcribed.
- Leibniz reference: `\unsure{\alpha)}`; alternatives `sign/extra close unresolved`. frame-left crosses candidate sign; one other close is clear
- compressed ordinal accent: `deuxi\unsure{è&#124;e}m`; alternatives `grave absent or merged`. one upper point could be displaced i dot
- compressed ordinal ending: `m\unsure{e&#124;.}`; alternatives `terminal e or abbreviation stop`. detached small crescent
- cancelled bracket: `\unsure{[X,Y]}`; alternatives `boundary brackets unresolved`. mask leaves X/comma/Y but no clean brackets
- definition abbreviation: `df\unsure{n}`; alternatives `df`. terminal shoulder could be f exit
